L1 · INFECTIOUS DISEASES
5-Step Framework (apply to every case)
① Identify lesion (vesicle/ulcer/plaque/papule/crust). ② Locate (oral mucosa vs skin vs both). ③ Determine acuity & systemic sx (fever, lymphadenopathy, malaise). ④ Risk factors (age, sexual hx, immune status, travel, contacts). ⑤ Diagnostic test & targeted tx. Cross-cutting: IgM = current/active · IgG = past/immune memory · "if it wipes off → Candida; if it doesn't → leukoplakia/lichenoid".
Impetigo: honey crusts over lip/perioral skin
Impetigo
MC bacterial skin infxn, 2–5 yr olds. Cause: Staph aureus + β-hemolytic strep. Honey-colored crusts ("cornflakes glued on"). Highly contagious (school outbreaks). Topical: mupirocin 2% TID ×5d. Systemic: Augmentin dose by WEIGHT not age — <40 kg = 25 mg/kg/d BID; ≥40 kg = 500 mg BID. Trap: don't dose by age in pediatrics.
Syphilis (3 stages — match stage to lesion)
Cause: Treponema pallidum — "cursed corkscrews" on bx (IHC preferred 64–94% sens; silver stain old method 0–41%). REPORTABLE all stages. "The Great Imitator". 6-wk serology window — RPR/VDRL may be falsely negative early in 1°.
PRIMARY: painless chancre 3–90 d post-exposure, heals 3–6 wk untreated. SECONDARY (~9 wk): mucous patches ("snail-track ulcers"), maculopapular rash palms+soles, condyloma lata, moth-eaten alopecia. TERTIARY (yrs later): gumma, interstitial glossitis, neuro/CV. CONGENITAL: Hutchinson triad (Hutchinson incisors, mulberry molars, interstitial keratitis), saddle nose, saber shins. Tx: Penicillin G IM (single dose primary; 3-dose tertiary). PCN-allergic: doxycycline.
Tuberculosis (TB)
Cause: Mycobacterium tuberculosis — acid-fast bacillus. REPORTABLE. Primary lung; oral lesion = chronic painful tongue ulcer (rare, posterior dorsal). Dx: PPD ≥10 mm (≥5 mm if HIV/contact), CXR Ghon focus, IGRA (QuantiFERON) if BCG-vaccinated. Tx: RIPE × 2 mo → RI × 4 mo (Rifampin · Isoniazid · Pyrazinamide · Ethambutol). Latent TB: 9 mo INH. Dental: defer elective tx until non-infectious (≥2 wk multidrug + neg sputum).
Candidiasis (6 subtypes)
Cause: Candida albicans. Wipes off = pseudomembranous; doesn't wipe = atrophic/erythematous. ① Pseudomembranous (thrush) — white wipe-off plaques. ② Erythematous/atrophic — red dorsal tongue, post-AB or steroid inhaler. ③ Median rhomboid glossitis — central depapillated rhomboid. ④ Angular cheilitis — corners of mouth, Candida + Staph. ⑤ Chronic hyperplastic — non-wipe-off, can be premalignant. ⑥ Mucocutaneous (CMC). 4 mandatory antifungals: clotrimazole troches (5×/d), nystatin susp (swish/swallow QID), miconazole gel/buccal tab, fluconazole 100–200 mg PO (×7–14d). Clotrimazole troche CI in diabetics (sucrose vehicle → caries/glycemic). Steroid-inhaler pts: rinse + spit after use.
HSV-1 / HSV-2 + Herpesviruses
8 HHVs: HHV-1 HSV-1 · HHV-2 HSV-2 · HHV-3 VZV · HHV-4 EBV · HHV-5 CMV · HHV-6/7 roseola · HHV-8 Kaposi. Primary HSV-1 = acute herpetic gingivostomatitis (ages 6 mo–5 yr): fever, crops of vesicles → ulcers on KERATINIZED + non-keratinized mucosa, dx clinical/Tzanck/PCR. Tx: supportive ± acyclovir 15 mg/kg 5×/d ×7d (start ≤5 d from onset). AVOID viscous lidocaine in children <3 (FDA boxed warning, seizures), avoid ASA in kids (Reye). Recurrent HSV-1 (RHL) = labial vermilion vesicles (cold sore) — prodromal tingling 24h, lasts 7–10d, attached gingiva & hard palate ONLY intraorally (keratinized only, vs aphthous = nonkerat). Tx: topical penciclovir 1% q2h start at 48-h prodrome; severe systemic → valacyclovir 1 g BID ×5d. HSV-2 = anogenital but cross-infect possible.
VZV (HHV-3) — Chickenpox & Shingles
Primary = varicella (chickenpox), recurrent = herpes zoster (shingles) — UNILATERAL, dermatomal, abrupt midline stop. Trigeminal V1 = Hutchinson sign (nose tip vesicle → ophthalmic involvement) — refer ophtho. V2/V3 = oral hemiface. Tx: valacyclovir 1 g TID ×7d if <72 hr. Postherpetic neuralgia common >60. Vaccine: Shingrix ≥50 yr.
CMV (HHV-5)
Owl-eye intranuclear inclusions on bx. #1 nonhereditary cause of SNHL in newborns. Oral = chronic deep punched-out ulcer in immunocompromised (HIV, transplant). Tx: valganciclovir. Pregnancy: TORCH screen.
Measles (Rubeola)
REPORTABLE → MA DPH. Koplik spots (blue-white grains opp upper molars) precede skin rash by 1–2 d = pathognomonic. 3 C's: Cough · Coryza · Conjunctivitis. Cephalocaudal rash. Airborne isolation until 4d after rash. MMR vaccine. Recent US outbreaks → high-yield.
Mumps
Paramyxovirus. Bilateral parotitis + fever + orchitis. 10–30% have NO parotid swelling (trap — don't rule out). Dx: RT-PCR buccal swab + IgM. Saliva test > serology in vaccinated. Reportable. MMR vaccine.
EBV (HHV-4) & EBV-MCU
Mono ("kissing dz"): fever + posterior cervical LAD + pharyngitis + splenomegaly. Avoid amox/ampicillin → maculopapular rash. EBV-MCU (mucocutaneous ulcer) = solitary indurated ulcer in immunosenescent/iatrogenic (elderly, MTX, biologics). DDx vs SCC vs lymphoma. Dx: EBER ISH. Discriminators (vs lymphoma): normal LDH · normal IL-2R · EBV DNA <1,000 copies/mL. Often self-resolves with immunosuppressant taper.
L1 Cross-cutting Traps
"Wipes off" rule → Candida pseudo (DDx: strep, herpangina); doesn't wipe → hyperplastic Candida (bx) OR leukoplakia/lichenoid. Keratinized vs non-kerat → recurrent HSV = kerat ONLY (att ging, hard palate); aphthous = non-kerat ONLY. Reportable: syphilis (all stages), TB, measles, mumps, HIV/AIDS → MA DPH. Augmentin = weight-based. Penicillin G = syphilis gold std. Antiviral windows: PrHGS acyclovir ≤5 d · RHL penciclovir at 48-h prodrome · zoster valacyclovir <72 h. No ASA <18, no viscous lidocaine <3. Mumps IgM may be falsely neg in vaccinated — RT-PCR is gold std. Hutchinson triad = congenital syphilis ≠ Hutchinson nasal-tip sign = V1 zoster. TB: never bx first — workup + airborne PPE + ID consult.
L2 · ALLERGIES & IMMUNOLOGIC
Hypersensitivity (4 types + pseudoallergic)
Type I (immediate, IgE) — anaphylaxis, urticaria, angioedema. Mast cell + histamine. Tx: epi 0.3 mg IM lat thigh. Type II (cytotoxic, IgG/IgM vs cell Ag) — pemphigus, MMP, hemolytic anemia. Type III (immune complex) — SLE, vasculitis, serum sickness. Type IV (delayed, T-cell, 24–72 h) — contact dermatitis, OLP, drug rxns, TB skin test. Pseudoallergic (non-IgE) — radiocontrast, NSAIDs, opioids → direct mast cell.
RAS (Recurrent Aphthous Stomatitis)
Non-keratinized mucosa ONLY (vs RHL keratinized). Yellow-gray pseudomembrane + erythematous halo. 3 variants: Minor (<1 cm, 7–10d, no scar, MC 80%) · Major (Sutton) (>1 cm, weeks-months, scars) · Herpetiform (50–100 pinpoint, coalesce). Complex aphthosis = ≥3 simultaneous OR continuous OR genital. Onset >40 = workup (Behçet, IBD, celiac, HIV, hematinic def — B12/folate/Fe/zinc). Tx ladder: topical clobetasol 0.05% > fluocinonide 0.05% > dex elixir 0.5 mg/5 mL swish/spit BID > triamcinolone in Orabase (trap — least potent). Severe: PO prednisone, colchicine, dapsone, apremilast.
Corticosteroid Pharmacotherapy (memorize ladder)
Ultra-high: clobetasol 0.05%. High: fluocinonide 0.05%. Dex elixir 0.5 mg/5 mL — universal first-line swish. Low (TRAP): triamcinolone in Orabase = weakest, do not lead with. Rules: vermilion border NEVER (atrophy/telangiectasia). Sensodyne SLS trap — SLS aggravates RAS, recommend SLS-free toothpastes: Cleure, Dr. Bob, Sensodyne ProNamel SLS-free, Biotene. Dex elixir Rx: 0.5 mg/5 mL, swish 5 mL ×2 min spit BID-QID ×2 wk.
Geographic Tongue (Benign Migratory Glossitis)
3 D's: Depapillated red patches · Demarcated white serpiginous border · Dynamic (migrates over days — rules out malignancy). Dorsal/lateral tongue. Type IV-like. Asymptomatic; some burning to acidic foods. Reassure. Variant: ectopic geographic stomatitis (extra-tongue). Assoc psoriasis.
Drug-Related Gingival Hyperplasia (DRGH)
3 drug classes: Phenytoin · Ca-channel blockers (nifedipine, amlodipine) · Cyclosporine. Onset 1–3 mo after start. Anterior labial gingiva first. Tx: meticulous plaque control ± gingivectomy ± drug switch (consult MD).
Transient Lingual Papillitis (TLP) — "lie bumps"
Painful red/white papules on fungiform papillae (tip + dorsolateral tongue). Resolves 2–15 days. Tx: reassurance first-line; bland diet, topical anesthetic if severe.
Plasma Cell Gingivitis & Cinnamomum Reaction (PCG)
Cinnamon flavoring (toothpaste, gum, candy) → cinnamomum reaction: lateral tongue + buccal mucosa erythema/erosion. Plasma cell gingivitis = bright red, sharply demarcated free + att gingiva. Tx: eliminate cinnamon.
Immunobullous Framework — Pegasus & Hades
Pegasus (intraepithelial, suprabasilar) = PV (DSG3/DSG1, IgG, "row of tombstones," chicken-wire DIF). Hades (subepithelial, BMZ) = MMP, BP, EBA, LABD (linear DIF along BMZ). Nikolsky sign: positive in PV, SJS/TEN, PNP; negative in MMP/BP (subepithelial tense roof), OLP, RAS, EM. DIF: PV chicken-wire intercellular IgG · MMP linear IgG/C3 BMZ · OLP shaggy fibrinogen BMZ.
OLP (Oral Lichen Planus)
Auto-INFLAMMATORY, NOT autoimmune (T-cell mediated). MC reticular (Wickham striae bilateral buccal); also erosive, plaque, atrophic, papular, bullous. 4 P's: Pruritic · Purple · Polygonal · Papules (skin). Wickham striae = white lacy bilateral buccal. OPMD controversy — ~1.4% MT risk (WHO classifies as OPMD; debated). Bx for atypical/erosive. Tx: clobetasol/fluocinonide; dex elixir; tacrolimus 0.1% if refractory.
MMP (Mucous Membrane Pemphigoid)
Subepithelial split, BMZ. Nikolsky NEGATIVE (tense subepithelial blister, thick roof). Desquamative gingivitis = #1 oral presentation. REFER ALL TO OPHTHALMOLOGY (symblepharon → blindness). Anti-laminin 332+ MMP → 25% occult adenocarcinoma — cancer screen mandatory. Biopsy 2-vial trap: perilesional (intact mucosa, NOT ulcer center) for DIF in Michel's medium; second specimen in formalin for H&E. "Don't Lose Your Sushi" = Don't bx ulcer Lesion, Use 2 vials, Yes perilesional, Submit Michel's. Tx: clobetasol/dex; refractory → dapsone, MMF, rituximab.
Pemphigus Vulgaris (PV)
IgG vs DSG3 (oral) ± DSG1 (skin). Acantholysis → "row of tombstones" basal cells. >50% present orally FIRST (months before skin). Painful flaccid bullae → erosions, Nikolsky+. DIF chicken-wire intercellular IgG. Severity: PDAI score. Tx: high-dose pred ± rituximab, MMF, AZA, IVIG. Untreated mortality high.
EM / RIME vs SJS / TEN
EM: target lesions, ~70% HSV-triggered (EM/RIME combined infection-triggered ~90%); EM minor <10% BSA + ≤1 mucosa, EM major ≥2 mucosa. RIME = post-infectious mucositis (Mycoplasma, COVID). SJS <10% BSA, SJS/TEN overlap 10–30%, TEN >30% BSA. Triggers: drugs >90% — allopurinol #1, sulfonamides, anticonvulsants (carbamazepine, lamotrigine, phenytoin), NSAIDs (oxicams). HLA-B*1502 + carbamazepine in Han Chinese/SE Asian → screen. SCORTEN mortality. Refer burn unit.
PNP / PAMS (Paraneoplastic Pemphigus)
Always = malignancy: NHL, CLL, Castleman dz, thymoma, Waldenström. Severe intractable stomatitis + polymorphous skin. Anti-plakin Ab (envoplakin, periplakin). Bronchiolitis obliterans → respiratory failure = leading cause of death. Tx: treat underlying tumor + immunosuppression.
L2 Cross-cutting Tables (high-yield)
Nikolsky+: PV, SJS/TEN, PNP. Nikolsky−: MMP/BP, OLP, RAS, EM. DIF chicken-wire = PV; linear BMZ = MMP/BP; shaggy fibrinogen = OLP; granular IgA dermal papillae = DH. BSA cutoffs: SJS <10 · overlap 10–30 · TEN >30. Histology: PV intraepithelial suprabasilar; MMP/BP subepithelial; OLP saw-tooth rete + band-like lymphocytes; EM apoptotic keratinocytes. Drug allergy: PCN cross-react cephalosporins ~1%; sulfa cross-react furosemide; NSAID cross-react ASA. Contact stomatitis: nickel, chromium, gold, mercury, balsam of Peru, cinnamaldehyde, eugenol. IBS vs IBD trap: IBS no oral findings; IBD (Crohn) = cobblestone, lip swelling, OFG, pyostomatitis vegetans, RAS-like.
Side B — L3 Epithelial/SCC + L4 Salivary
SIDE B — L3 EPITHELIAL PATHOSES & SCC (green) + L4 SALIVARY GLAND (purple) | rates, staging, anatomy
L3 · EPITHELIAL PATHOSES & SCC
Foundations
Pathosis = the disease/lesion; pathology = the study. WHO 2017: OPMD = oral potentially malignant disorder (replaces "premalignant"). Dysplasia = architectural + cytologic atypia confined above BM; CIS = full-thickness atypia; SCC = invasion through BM. Visual exam: ~60% sens, ~62% spec — bx is gold std.
Epi / Risk
US 2024: ~36,600 new oral/oropharyngeal cancers, ~12,230 deaths. M:F 2–4:1. Tongue (lateral) = 41.7% oral cavity site. Risk: tobacco × alcohol = 30× multiplicative (not additive). Fanconi anemia OR 40.6×. Other: betel/areca, immunosuppression, prior cancer, UV (lip vermilion). Lip vermilion = separate dz (UV-driven, lower lip).
HPV Benign Lesions (don't confuse with SCC)
Squamous papilloma — HPV 6, 11, single cauliflower. Condyloma acuminatum — HPV 6, 11, STI, multiple. Verruca vulgaris — HPV 2, 4, autoinoc fingers→lip. Heck disease (focal epithelial hyperplasia) — HPV 13, 32, multiple soft pink papules, NOT covered by Gardasil 9 (TRAP). Gardasil 9 = HPV 6/11/16/18/31/33/45/52/58.
Leukoplakia & Malignant Transformation
Definition: CLINICAL term, not histologic — white plaque that cannot be wiped or scraped (unlike pseudomembranous Candida); bx returns "hyperkeratosis," never "leukoplakia." MT by morphology: homogeneous ~2% · non-homogeneous 15–25% · verrucous/PVL 29–50%. MT by dysplasia grade (separate stat): no dysp 2.2% · mild 2.6–11.9% · mod 4.1–8.7% · severe/CIS 29.2–32.2%. 39.6% paradox: 39.6% of leukoplakias that progressed to SCC had NO dysplasia on initial biopsy. Bx every clinically suspicious lesion.
Erythroplakia (highest single MT)
Velvety red plaque. ~50% MT. 75–90% already dysplasia/CIS/SCC at biopsy. Mean 3.7 mo from dx → cancer. Floor of mouth, soft palate, ventral tongue = highest-risk sites. Bx always, urgently.
Proliferative Verrucous Leukoplakia (PVL)
48–50% MT, ~8× lifetime risk vs leukoplakia. F > M, often non-smokers. Gingiva "ring around the collar" = signature. Multifocal, recurrent despite tx. Cerero/Villa criteria. Long-term surveillance — recurrence rule, not exception.
OPMD ranking (highest→lowest MT)
① PVL (~48–50%, highest of any OPMD) ② Erythroplakia (~50% but smaller cohort) ③ Erythroleukoplakia ④ OSF (~5.1%) ⑤ Verrucous leukoplakia ⑥ Non-homogeneous leukoplakia ⑦ Homogeneous leukoplakia ⑧ Actinic cheilitis ⑨ OLP (1.4%, debated OPMD).
Oral Submucous Fibrosis (OSF)
Areca/betel nut chewers (S Asian). Trismus, blanched mucosa, fibrous bands buccal. 5.1% MT, mean 36 mo. IRREVERSIBLE once established. EMT cascade drives fibrosis + transformation. Stop nut, intralesional steroids/hyaluronidase, mouth-opening exercises.
Biopsy Rules (memorize)
"Red over white, edge over center, deep for verrucous, multiple for big." Sample most clinically alarming area (red > white, indurated). Edge = perilesional includes normal-abnormal junction. Verrucous = deep wedge (surface bx misses invasion). Lesions >1.5 cm → ≥2 sites. Tumor seeding from biopsy = MYTH (do not delay bx for fear of seeding).
Dysplasia Management
Mild → observe + risk-factor mod ± laser. Moderate–severe/CIS → excision. Modalities: scalpel OR 3.6, CO2 laser HR 0.14 (best recurrence ↓), diode 93.5% CR, cryotherapy 100% CR, ALA-PDT 66–100% CR. Re-bx if recur.
Chemoprevention (background only)
Imiquimod (TLR-7 agonist) — topical, mucosal trials. Beta-carotene CARET trap: ↑lung cancer in smokers, do NOT use. RP2 trial (BWH/Dana-Farber) — ongoing.
Oral SCC
>90% of oral malignancies. Mean age 66. M:F 2–4:1. Site MC lateral tongue 42% > FOM > soft palate. Induration = #1 sign. 5 warning signs: ulcer >2 wk · induration · fixed lymph node · pain/dysesthesia · loose tooth/poorly fitting denture. Bx mandatory if persistent.
AJCC 8th — what changed
DOI added to T-staging: T1 ≤2 cm AND DOI ≤5 mm · T2 ≤4 cm AND DOI ≤10 mm · T3 >4 cm OR DOI >10 mm · T4 invades adj structures. ENE added to N-stage: ENE+ upstages. N1 single ipsi ≤3 cm ENE− · N2a single ipsi 3–6 cm ENE− OR ≤3 cm ENE+ · N3b ENE+. Stage I → IVC. DOI = depth of invasion from BM, NOT tumor thickness.
HPV-OPSCC (oropharynx only)
Tonsil + base of tongue. HPV-16 in ~90%. 5-yr OS HPV+ ~80–85% vs HPV− ~40%. p16 IHC = surrogate for HPV — reliable in oropharynx ONLY, NOT oral cavity. E6 → p53 inactivation; E7 → Rb inactivation. Gardasil 9 covers 9 types (6/11/16/18/31/33/45/52/58). M>F, oral sex, younger pts.
Carcinogenesis Cascade
Normal → hyperplasia → mild → moderate → severe dysplasia → CIS → invasive SCC. Field cancerization = whole upper aerodigestive carcinogen-exposed; 2nd primary risk 3–7%/yr. ~17% of OPMD progress; remainder regress or stable.
Verrucous Carcinoma
Low-grade SCC variant. White cauliflower, slow, 0% metastasis. Wide pushing border (NOT infiltrative — that's classic SCC). Deep wedge bx mandatory — superficial bx misses dx (calls it "verrucous hyperplasia"). Surgical excision; avoid RT (anaplastic transformation risk).
Oral Melanoma
~50% on hard palate. UV NOT cause (intraoral). ~15% amelanotic (NOT 25%) — pink/red, easy miss. Mol: c-KIT mutations → imatinib; BRAF mutations rare (vs cutaneous). Poor prognosis, often advanced at dx. Bx any pigmented oral lesion that is new, growing, multifocal, or asymmetric.
L4 · SALIVARY GLAND DISORDERS
Anatomy & Output
3 majors: parotid (serous, Stensen duct opp upper 2nd molar), submandibular (mixed, Wharton duct floor of mouth, ~70% resting saliva), sublingual (mucous, Rivinus ducts). 600–1000 minor glands (lip, palate, buccal). Tubarial glands = "4th pair," Valstar 2021 (Radiother Oncol), torus tubarius. Waterfall mnemonic: parotid → over masseter → through buccinator → Stensen at upper 2M (everything flows down).
10 Functions of Saliva
① Lubrication ② Antimicrobial (lysozyme, lactoferrin, IgA) ③ Buffering (bicarbonate) ④ Remineralization (Ca, PO4, F) ⑤ Digestion (amylase) ⑥ Taste solubilization ⑦ Mucosal protection (mucins) ⑧ Speech/swallowing ⑨ Hormone barrier (kallikrein) ⑩ Wound healing (EGF, NGF).
Hyposalivation vs Xerostomia (different!)
Hyposalivation = OBJECTIVE flow ↓. Xerostomia = SUBJECTIVE dry-mouth complaint. Cutoffs: unstim ≤0.1 mL/min · stim ≤0.5–0.7 mL/min. Normal unstim 0.3–0.4, stim 1–2. ~16–27% overlap (some xerostomia w/ normal flow; some hyposal w/o complaint).
Challacombe Scale (10 items, score 1–10)
Mild 1–3: mirror sticks to mucosa (item 2) · loss of palatal rugae · frothy saliva. Moderate 4–6: tongue lobulated/fissured · altered gingival architecture · glassy mucosa appearance. Severe 7–10: active dental decay · debris on palate / sticking to teeth · item 9 cervical caries (>2 teeth) · item 10 debris on palate or sticking to teeth.
Causes (ranked)
Top 3: anxiety · polypharmacy >3 meds/day (OR 2.9) · dehydration. Then: anticholinergics, antihistamines, antidepressants (TCAs, SSRIs), antihypertensives, opioids, diuretics, chemo, head/neck RT, Sjögren, diabetes (uncontrolled), HIV, hepatitis C. OR table: anticholinergics ~3–4×, polypharmacy ~2.9×.
Radiation Caries (head/neck RT)
6 features: cervical & incisal edges · rapid progression · circumferential · brown-black · pulp involvement early · symmetric. Thresholds: parotid >25 Gy → permanent xerostomia; submandibular >39 Gy; >65–70 Gy → ORN risk. Tx: F⁻ trays 1.1% NaF nightly, CPP-ACP, sialagogues, fastidious recall q3mo, pre-RT extractions.
Sialagogues — M3 Pharmacology
Pilocarpine — non-selective M-agonist, 5 mg PO TID-QID. Cevimeline — M1+M3, 30 mg TID. Bethanechol — M2+M3 (off-label). CIs: narrow-angle glaucoma, uncontrolled asthma, severe COPD/CHF, cardiac disease, GI/GU obstruction. SE: sweating, GI cramps, urinary urgency, miosis, bradycardia. Ramadan dosing: pilocarpine pre-suhoor + post-iftar to comply with fasting.
Avoid / Use Rules (DIE ON THIS HILL)
Vaseline = NO on lips (petrolatum sits on surface, no hydration, dries the dry). Lanolin (HPA-Lanolin) = YES — biomimetic. Avoid alcohol mouthwash (Listerine red), avoid SLS. Use: Biotene, Xylimelts at bedtime, OraCoat, ACT dry mouth, sip water, sugar-free gum (xylitol). Humidifier at night.
Sjögren Syndrome (ACR-EULAR 2016)
Score ≥4 = classification. Items: labial salivary gland bx focus score ≥1 (=3 pts), anti-SSA/Ro+ (=3), ocular staining ≥5 (=1), Schirmer ≤5 mm/5 min (=1), unstim flow ≤0.1 mL/min (=1), SSB+ (=1, cannot reach ≥4 alone). Exclusions: HCV, HIV, sarcoid, amyloid, GVHD, IgG4-RD, head/neck RT. ~30% seronegative. F:M 9:1. Primary vs secondary (other CTD). 5–10% lifetime MALT lymphoma risk, 7–19× RR. Red flags: persistent unilateral parotid swelling, fixed mass, B sx, ↑LDH, low C4. Bilateral fluctuant = reassuring.
Sialolithiasis (Stones)
Submandibular 80–90% (Wharton, mucous, alkaline, gravity). Meal-time pain & swelling = pathognomonic ("mealtime syndrome"). 3 mechanisms: salivary stasis · sialomicrolith nidus · ductal inflammation. Occlusal radiograph FIRST; CT if non-radiopaque. Sialendoscopy 85–92% success; bimanual milking; conservative tx for small distal stones.
Bacterial Sialadenitis
S. aureus #1; also strep, anaerobes. Risk: dehydration, post-op, hyposalivation. Acute parotid swelling, pus from Stensen on milking. Tx: Augmentin OR clindamycin, hydration, sialagogues, warm compresses, gland massage. ED criteria: airway compromise, sepsis, abscess.
Mucocele & Ranula
Mucocele: lower lip 60–70%, lip-biting trauma. Extravasation (no epithelial lining, MC) vs retention (lined, less common). Bluish dome, fluctuant, recurrent. Tx: excise WITH feeding minor gland (otherwise recurs). Ranula = FOM mucocele from sublingual gland; "frog belly." Plunging ranula dissects through mylohyoid → neck mass. Tx: marsupialization or sublingual gland excision (definitive).
Salivary Gland Tumors (gland-size paradox)
Larger gland = more benign; smaller gland = more malignant. Parotid ~80% benign; submand ~50/50; sublingual + minor >80% malignant. Pleomorphic adenoma = MC benign (parotid tail), painless mobile mass, low MT to carcinoma-ex-PA. Warthin tumor = papillary cystadenoma lymphomatosum, smokers, M>F, often bilateral parotid. Mucoepidermoid carcinoma = MC malignant, palate in minor glands, low/intermediate/high grade. Adenoid cystic = perineural invasion, late mets. FNA + MRI for any persistent salivary mass.
Master Strip — Cross-Lecture Synthesis (gold)
MASTER STRIP — pathognomonic anchors · oral=first sign · avoid/use rules · drug cross-reference · reportable · mnemonics · prof-emphasis · numbers · DDx · QA corrections
PATHOGNOMONIC ANCHORS (one-shot ID)
Honey-colored crusts → impetigo. Cursed corkscrews on silver stain → T. pallidum. Chancre painless ulcer → 1° syphilis. Mucous patches / snail-track + palms/soles rash → 2° syphilis. Hutchinson incisors + mulberry molars → congenital syphilis. Wipes off white plaque → pseudomembranous candidiasis. Owl-eye intranuclear inclusions → CMV. Koplik spots (blue-white opp upper molars, precede rash 1–2 d) → measles. Hutchinson sign nose-tip vesicle → V1 zoster ophthalmicus. Crops of vesicles on keratinized + non-keratinized → primary HSV. EBER ISH+ solitary indurated ulcer in immunosuppressed → EBV-MCU.

Wickham striae bilateral buccal → OLP. Row of tombstones + chicken-wire DIF → PV. Desquamative gingivitis + linear IgG/C3 BMZ → MMP. Target lesions + HSV trigger → EM. Bronchiolitis obliterans + stomatitis + malignancy → PNP. Cobblestone mucosa + lip swelling + cheilitis granulomatosa → Crohn/OFG.

Velvety red plaque FOM → erythroplakia. Gingiva "ring around the collar" → PVL. Trismus + blanched buccal bands + areca chewer → OSF. Multiple soft pink papules in child, Inuit/Native → Heck (HPV 13/32). Cauliflower exophytic mass, no metastasis → verrucous CA. Pigmented patch on hard palate, growing → oral melanoma.

Mealtime swelling/pain submand floor → sialolith. Bilateral parotid + dry eyes/mouth + SSA+ → Sjögren. FOM "frog belly" cystic swelling → ranula. Lower lip dome, recurs after pop → mucocele. Smoker, bilateral parotid mass → Warthin. Painless mobile parotid tail mass → pleomorphic adenoma.
ORAL = FIRST SIGN (don't miss these)
Pemphigus Vulgaris — >50% present orally first, months before skin. Painful flaccid bullae, Nikolsky+.
MMP — desquamative gingivitis often only finding for years. REFER OPHTHO all cases (symblepharon → blindness).
Crohn disease (IBD) — oral cobblestone, lip swelling, OFG, pyostomatitis vegetans, RAS-like ulcers can precede GI sx.
PNP / PAMS — severe intractable stomatitis = clue to underlying NHL/CLL/Castleman/thymoma.
EBV-MCU — solitary deep ulcer in immunosenescent (elderly on MTX/biologics) = first sign of dysregulation; often resolves with immunosuppressant taper.
Behçet — recurrent oral aphthae triad with genital ulcers + uveitis.
SLE — oral discoid lesions on hard palate.
Sarcoidosis — granulomatous lip swelling.
Sjögren — xerostomia + bilateral parotid swelling can precede sicca/ANA workup. Bx labial gland diagnostic.
Syphilis 2° — mucous patches often noticed on dental exam before systemic dx.
HIV — recurrent candidiasis (esp pseudomemb), HSV, HPV, NUG/NUP, Kaposi sarcoma palate.
Leukemia — gingival hyperplasia (esp AML M4/M5), spontaneous bleeding, petechiae.
Vit deficiency — angular cheilitis (Fe), atrophic glossitis (B12), bleeding gums (C).
Anorexia/bulimia — perimylolysis (palatal erosion), parotid swelling.
EM/RIME — hemorrhagic crusted lips + target oral lesions, often before skin (or only mucosa).
AVOID RULES (exam traps disguised as common practice)
Vermilion border NEVER apply potent steroids (clobetasol/fluocinonide) → atrophy/telangiectasia.
Vaseline on lips = NO in xerostomia (occlusive only, no biomimicry). Use HPA-Lanolin YES.
Sensodyne with SLS aggravates RAS — switch to SLS-free Sensodyne ProNamel / Biotene.
Listerine alcohol in xerostomia — drying. Use alcohol-free (Biotene rinse, ACT dry mouth).
Triamcinolone in Orabase = weakest, do not lead with — exam expects clobetasol/dex elixir.
Clotrimazole troche CI in diabetics (sucrose vehicle).
Amoxicillin/ampicillin in mono (EBV) → near-100% maculopapular rash.
ASA in children <18 with viral illness → Reye syndrome.
Viscous lidocaine in children <3 → FDA boxed warning, seizures/death.
Beta-carotene in smokers (CARET trial) → ↑lung cancer.
Carbamazepine in Han Chinese / SE Asian without HLA-B*1502 screen → SJS/TEN.
RT to verrucous CA → anaplastic transformation; surgical excision instead.
Sialagogues in narrow-angle glaucoma, uncontrolled asthma, severe COPD/CHF, cardiac disease, GI/GU obstruction → CI.
Bx ulcer center alone for immunobullous → false negative DIF; sample perilesional in Michel's.
Tumor seeding from biopsy = MYTH; never delay biopsy of suspicious oral lesion.
p16 IHC in oral cavity SCC = unreliable; only valid in oropharynx.
Empiric AB before bx of bacterial sialadenitis if no culture? — clinical (S. aureus most likely) is fine; abscess drain.
DRUG CROSS-REFERENCE (4-lecture grid)
L1 INFECTIOUS: Augmentin (impetigo, weight-based; sialadenitis); PCN G IM (syphilis gold std); Doxycycline (PCN-allergic syphilis); RIPE (TB 2 mo) → RI (TB 4 mo); INH 9 mo latent TB; Fluconazole 100–200 mg (Candida 7–14d); Clotrimazole troche 5×/d (NOT diabetics); Nystatin susp; Miconazole gel/buccal; Acyclovir 15 mg/kg 5×/d ×7d primary HSV; Penciclovir 1% q2h RHL; Valacyclovir 1 g TID ×7d zoster; Valganciclovir CMV.

L2 ALLERGIES/IMMUNO: Clobetasol 0.05% ultra-high; Fluocinonide 0.05% high; Dex elixir 0.5 mg/5 mL swish/spit BID-QID universal; Triamcinolone in Orabase low (trap); Tacrolimus 0.1% refractory OLP; Prednisone PO severe; Dapsone MMP/dermatitis herpetiformis; Colchicine, apremilast RAS; Rituximab, MMF, AZA, IVIG PV refractory; Epi 0.3 mg IM lat thigh anaphylaxis. Avoid: SLS, alcohol mw, vermilion potent steroids.

L3 EPITHELIAL/SCC: CO2 laser dysplasia (HR 0.14); Diode laser 93.5% CR; Cryotherapy 100% CR; ALA-PDT 66–100% CR; Imiquimod (TLR-7) chemoprev trial; Beta-carotene NO in smokers (CARET); Cisplatin/cetuximab/pembrolizumab SCC systemic; Imatinib c-KIT+ oral melanoma. Gardasil 9 (6/11/16/18/31/33/45/52/58) — HPV-OPSCC prevention but NOT Heck (13/32).

L4 SALIVARY: Pilocarpine 5 mg PO TID-QID (non-selective M); Cevimeline 30 mg TID (M1+M3); Bethanechol (M2+M3, off-label); F⁻ 1.1% NaF tray nightly + CPP-ACP rad caries; Augmentin or clindamycin bacterial sialadenitis; Xylitol gum, Xylimelts, Biotene rinse, OraCoat, ACT dry mouth; HPA-Lanolin lips. CIs sialagogues = narrow-angle glaucoma, uncontrolled asthma, severe COPD/CHF, cardiac disease, GI/GU obstruction.
REPORTABLE DISEASES & REFERRAL TRIGGERS
Mandatory report → MA DPH: Syphilis (all stages), Tuberculosis (active & latent in some categories), Measles (rubeola), Mumps, HIV/AIDS, hepatitis A/B/C (acute), pertussis, meningococcal dz.

Refer ophthalmology IMMEDIATELY: MMP (all) — symblepharon → blindness · V1 zoster (Hutchinson sign) · severe Sjögren keratoconjunctivitis sicca.

Refer dermatology / oral med: PV, PNP, refractory OLP, suspected EM/SJS, DRESS.
Refer OMFS / oral med: any persistent salivary mass (FNA + MRI), submandibular stone refractory to sialendoscopy, suspected SCC, mucocele recurrence.
Refer rheumatology: ACR-EULAR ≥4 Sjögren, RAS >40 with workup positive (Behçet, IBD), suspected SLE/Crohn.
Refer ED / burn unit: SJS/TEN (BSA, SCORTEN), anaphylaxis, deep neck space infection, severe sialadenitis with airway compromise.
Refer hematology/oncology: PNP (always malignancy), MALT lymphoma red flags (unilateral persistent parotid mass, fixed, B sx, ↑LDH).
MNEMONICS MASTER LIST
L1: 3 C's of measles = Cough · Coryza · Conjunctivitis. Hutchinson triad (congenital syphilis) = Hutchinson incisors · mulberry molars · interstitial keratitis. RIPE TB intensive = Rifampin · Isoniazid · Pyrazinamide · Ethambutol → RI continuation. HHV 1–8: HSV1 · HSV2 · VZV · EBV · CMV · HHV6/7 roseola · HHV8 Kaposi. 5-step framework: lesion → location → acuity/sx → risk → test/tx.

L2: Pegasus & Hades — Pegasus intraepithelial (PV row of tombstones, chicken wire DIF); Hades subepithelial BMZ (MMP, BP, EBA, LABD, linear DIF). 3 D's geographic tongue: Depapillated · Demarcated white border · Dynamic (migrates). 4 P's lichen planus: Pruritic · Purple · Polygonal · Papules. Don't Lose Your Sushi = Don't bx ulcer · Lesion perilesional · Use 2 vials · Yes 2nd specimen · Sushi=Submit Michel's medium for DIF. "Doctor Bob" dex elixir 0.5 mg/5 mL swish 2 min spit BID-QID ×2 wk.

L3: Biopsy rules: "Red over white, edge over center, deep for verrucous, multiple for big." OPMD ranking mnemonic: ER > PVL > ELP > OSF > verrucous LK > non-hom LK > hom LK > AC > OLP > DLE. 5 SCC warning signs: ulcer >2 wk · induration · fixed node · pain/dysesthesia · loose tooth/poor denture fit. DOI not thickness for AJCC 8.

L4: Waterfall parotid → masseter → buccinator → Stensen at upper 2M. 10 saliva functions: Lube · Antimicrobial · Buffer · Remin · Digest · Taste · Mucosal · Speech · Hormone · Wound. Gland-size paradox: bigger = benign, smaller = malignant. Mealtime swelling = stone. Ramadan sialagogue dosing pre-suhoor + post-iftar.

CROSS: IgM = current, IgG = past. "Wipes off" rule = Candida vs leukoplakia/lichenoid. Keratinized vs non-kerat: RHL kerat only · aphthous non-kerat only.
PROF-EMPHASIS VERBATIM (high-yield quotes)
L1: "Augmentin is dosed by weight, not age." · "If it wipes off, it's Candida; if it doesn't, it's leukoplakia or lichenoid." · "Hutchinson sign on the nose tip means the eye is next." · "Koplik spots precede the rash by 1–2 days — they are pathognomonic." · "Treponema pallidum looks like cursed corkscrews on silver stain." · "Syphilis is The Great Imitator — every stage." · "EBV-MCU in immunosenescent patients — bx and check EBER, often resolves with immunosuppressant taper."

L2: "Recurrent HSV is keratinized only; aphthous is non-keratinized only — that's how you tell them apart." · "Vermilion border, NEVER potent steroids." · "SLS in toothpaste flares RAS — ask the patient what toothpaste they use." · "If you suspect MMP, you refer to ophthalmology, no exception." · "Pemphigus vulgaris presents in the mouth FIRST in more than half of patients." · "Allopurinol is the #1 cause of SJS/TEN in 2024."

L3: "Induration is the #1 sign of SCC — feel the lesion." · "Erythroplakia > leukoplakia for malignant transformation — it's red, it's bad, biopsy now." · "Don't be afraid to biopsy. Tumor seeding is a myth." · "DOI is in AJCC 8 — depth of invasion from BM, not tumor thickness." · "p16 in oropharynx is reliable; in oral cavity it is NOT." · "HPV-OPSCC has 80–85% 5-yr OS — it behaves differently."

L4: "Vaseline on lips? I will die on this hill — NO. Use HPA-Lanolin." · "Hyposalivation is objective; xerostomia is subjective — they are not the same word." · [Standard teaching, not prof verbatim:] "Submandibular gland makes ~70% of resting saliva — that's why it matters." · "Bigger gland = benign; smaller gland = malignant." · "Mealtime swelling = stone until proven otherwise."
NUMBERS FACT BANK (memorize the digits)
EPI: 36,600 new oral/oropharyngeal cancers · 12,230 deaths · 41.7% lateral tongue · M:F 2.4:1. Tobacco × alcohol = 30× multiplicative. Fanconi anemia OR 40.6×. ~17% OPMD progress. 2nd primary 3–7%/yr.

MT BY OPMD: PVL 48–50% (highest) · Erythroplakia 50% · OSF 5.1% · Homog leuko ~2% · Non-hom leuko 15–25% · OLP 1.4%. MT BY DYSPLASIA GRADE (separate stat) (different stat): no dysp 2.2% · mild 2.6–11.9% · moderate 4.1–8.7% · severe/CIS 29.2–32.2%. 39.6% paradox: 39.6% of leukoplakias that progressed to SCC had NO dysplasia on initial bx. 75–90% erythroplakia already dysplasia/CIS at bx. Erythroplakia mean 3.7 mo to cancer.

STAGING (AJCC 8): T1 ≤2 cm + DOI ≤5 mm · T2 ≤4 cm AND DOI ≤10 mm · T3 >4 cm OR DOI >10 · T4 invades adj. N1 single ipsi ≤3 cm ENE− · N2a 3–6 cm ENE− OR ≤3 cm ENE+ · N3b ENE+. DOI added · ENE upstages.

HPV: HPV-16 in ~90% HPV-OPSCC. HPV+ 5-yr OS ~80–85% vs HPV− ~40%. Heck HPV 13/32 NOT in Gardasil 9. Gardasil 9 covers 6/11/16/18/31/33/45/52/58.

SALIVARY: Submand 80–90% of stones · 70% resting saliva. Parotid >25 Gy permanent xerostomia · Submand >39 Gy · ORN risk >65–70 Gy. Sialendoscopy 85–92% success. Hyposal cutoff: unstim ≤0.1 · stim ≤0.5–0.7. Normal unstim 0.3–0.4 · stim 1–2. Sjögren ACR-EULAR ≥4: bx focus ≥1 = 3 pts, SSA = 3, others 1. ~30% Sjögren seronegative. F:M 9:1. MALT 5–10% lifetime, 7–19× RR. Tubarial glands 4th pair 2020.

L1/L2: Impetigo 2–5 yr olds · Augmentin <40 kg = 25 mg/kg/d BID, ≥40 kg = 500 mg BID · acyclovir 15 mg/kg 5×/d ×7d primary HSV, valacyclovir 1 g TID ×7d zoster · PCN G IM single dose 1° syphilis. RAS minor <1 cm, major Sutton >1 cm. SJS <10% BSA · overlap 10–30% · TEN >30%. EM 90% HSV-trigger. Drug onset of DRGH 1–3 mo. PV mortality untreated very high.
DDx MINI-CARDS (presentation → top differentials)
Painless ulcer >2 wk → SCC #1 · primary syphilitic chancre · TB ulcer · NHL/EBV-MCU · deep fungal · traumatic ulcer (rule out by removing irritant). Bx mandatory if persists.

Painful necrotic deep ulcer in immunocompromised → CMV · EBV-MCU · deep fungal (mucor, histo) · NUG/NUP · lymphoma · vasculitis (GPA). Bx + cultures + EBER + viral PCR.

White plaque "wipes off" → pseudomembranous candidiasis (clotrim/nystatin/flucon). Doesn't wipe → leukoplakia, OLP, frictional, hairy leukoplakia (EBV in HIV), white sponge nevus, leukoedema.

Multiple oral vesicles in child <5 → primary HSV gingivostomatitis · HFMD (Coxsackie A) · varicella · enteroviral herpangina (soft palate only).

Bilateral parotid swelling → Sjögren · mumps · sialosis (alcoholism, bulimia, diabetes) · HIV-DILS · sarcoid · IgG4-RD · Warthin (smoker, often bilateral). Reassuring if bilateral fluctuant; red flag if unilateral persistent fixed.

Unilateral persistent parotid mass → pleomorphic adenoma · Warthin (smoker) · mucoepidermoid CA · adenoid cystic CA (perineural pain) · MALT lymphoma in Sjögren · metastasis. FNA + MRI.

Desquamative gingivitis → MMP > OLP > PV > LABD > chronic ulcerative stomatitis. Perilesional bx + DIF.

Target lesions + lip crusts → EM (HSV) · RIME · SJS overlap (BSA, drugs). Drug + BSA >10% = SJS, refer.

Pigmented hard palate patch → oral melanoma · racial pigmentation · amalgam tattoo · smoker's melanosis · drug-induced (minocycline, OCP) · post-inflammatory · nevus. Bx if growing/asymmetric/multifocal.

Recurrent oral aphthae → simple RAS · complex aphthosis · Behçet · IBD (Crohn/UC) · celiac · HIV · cyclic neutropenia · hematinic def (Fe, B12, folate, zinc). Workup if onset >40 or complex.

Lower lip dome cystic → mucocele (extravasation MC) · retention cyst · salivary gland tumor (uncommon lower lip — minor gland tumors typically palate). FOM same → ranula.

Mealtime swelling → sialolithiasis (submand 80–90%) · ductal stenosis · juvenile recurrent parotitis · bacterial sialadenitis. Occlusal XR first.
QA-VERIFIED CORRECTIONS (don't fall for old slides)
Oral melanoma amelanotic = ~15%, NOT 25% (older texts cite 25; current literature 15%).
HPV− OPSCC 5-yr OS ~40%, NOT 50–55% (NCDB updated).
Tobacco + alcohol = 30× multiplicative, NOT additive. Don't add the OR's.
p16 IHC reliable in oropharynx ONLY. In oral cavity SCC, p16 does NOT correlate with HPV — do NOT use to drive tx.
PVL ~48–50% MT, F > M, often non-smokers — old slides may say "smoker." Updated: gingival "ring around the collar" classic.
Heck disease HPV 13/32 NOT in Gardasil 9 — common trap.
Verrucous CA: 0% metastasis, defining feature is wide pushing border (NOT infiltrative); needs deep wedge bx; superficial misses dx as "verrucous hyperplasia."
Tumor seeding from biopsy = MYTH — never delay bx.
DOI > tumor thickness for AJCC 8 — measure from BM.
OLP MT ~1.4%, debated; classified OPMD by WHO but lower than other OPMDs.
EBV-MCU in immunosenescent often resolves with immunosuppressant taper — don't aggressively excise; bx + observe.
Triamcinolone in Orabase = LOW potency trap — exam expects clobetasol/dex elixir as first-line.
Mumps 10–30% have NO parotid swelling — don't rule out by absence; RT-PCR buccal swab.
Vaseline NO, Lanolin YES for xerostomia lips — Vaseline is occlusive only, no biomimicry.
Erythroplakia 50% MT but 75–90% already dysplasia/CIS at biopsy — different stat, both true.
Sjögren ~30% seronegative — bx gold std (focus score ≥1, 3 pts in ACR-EULAR).
Submandibular gland 70% resting saliva, parotid dominates only on stimulation.
Allopurinol is the #1 trigger of SJS/TEN currently — older lists say sulfa first.
Carbamazepine + Han Chinese / SE Asian → screen HLA-B*1502 first — FDA boxed warning.
PV chicken-wire DIF intercellular IgG; MMP linear BMZ IgG/C3; OLP shaggy fibrinogen BMZ — keep these straight.

AVOID Rules / Dental Red-Lines

"Never do this" master list for L1
  • TB suspected: NEVER biopsy first — workup + airborne PPE + ID referral first.
  • Measles: NEVER treat in dental clinic — N-95 + negative-pressure room mandatory.
  • Nasal-tip HSV/VZV (Hutchinson sign): mandatory ophthalmology referral — HZO/blindness risk.
  • Topical lidocaine in kids: avoid — seizure risk.
  • Topical benzocaine in kids: avoid — methemoglobinemia.
  • CMV in HIV+: acyclovir will FAIL — use valganciclovir 900 mg PO BID.
  • EBV MCU: biopsy alone is insufficient — add EBER ISH to distinguish from lymphoma.
  • Augmentin dosing: use WEIGHT not age — cutoff is 40 kg, not "child vs adult."
  • Syphilis ≤ 6 wk: serology is unreliable — biopsy with IHC.
  • Candida "-azoles": check LFTs for all (clotrimazole ↑ LFTs ~15%; fluconazole rare hepatotox).
  • Inhaled-steroid candida: rinse + spacer — both required, both highlighted.
  • Mumps in vaccinated patient: IgM may be falsely negative — do RT-PCR from buccal swab.

"Oral = First Sign" — The Dentist's Diagnostic Window

When the mouth gets you to the diagnosis before the medical workup catches up
  • Koplik spots appear 1–2 days BEFORE the measles rash — you may diagnose measles before the pediatrician.
  • Chancre on lateral tongue — primary syphilis often presents to dentistry first (4% of chancres are oral).
  • Mucous patches + split papules — secondary syphilis before the patient knows they have a systemic illness.
  • Oral CMV ulcer in HIV+ off ART — reactivation marker; failed acyclovir is the case clue.
  • EBV MCU — marker of immunosuppression in transplant / iatrogenic / age-related populations.
  • Keratinized vesicle-ulcers in a 6-mo-to-5-yr child — PrHGS, often the family's first encounter with HSV.
  • White plaques that wipe off after inhaler use or antibiotic course — pseudomembranous candida (early diagnostic clue for inhaler misuse).
  • Large irregular painful tongue ulcer + night sweats + cough — TB, not SCC (TB has no raised, induced borders).

PART II — Printable Notecard (5.5″ × 9″, double-sided)

Pre-extracted forgettable details for handwritten transcription with 0.2mm color-coded pens (RED = L1 Infectious Diseases)
HOW TO USE

This notecard is sized 5.5″ × 9″ double-sided for handwritten transcription. Pen color code: RED = L1 Infectious Diseases, BLUE = L2 (TBD), GREEN = L3 (TBD), PURPLE = L4 (TBD). Yellow highlights = Henderson's exact highlighted facts (direct exam questions).

OD531 OM MIDTERM SIDE A  |  L1=INFECTIOUS DISEASES  |  L2=TBD  |  L3=TBD  |  L4=TBD  |  Abbrev: LAD=lymphadenopathy DOT=directly observed tx IHC=immunohistochem LFT=liver fxn test HZO=herpes zoster ophthalmicus ART=antiretroviral tx MCU=mucocutaneous ulcer EBER=EBV-encoded RNA ISH=in situ hybridization DPH=Dept Public Health

Impetigo

  • Staph aureus / β-hemolytic strep. Most common bacterial skin infxn in 2–5 yo (case was 7yo)
  • Risk triad: Broken skin + Runny nose + Warm moist climate
  • Lesion: Honey-colored crusts = "Cornflakes glued to surface"; multifocal on erythematous base
  • vs Herpes: Impetigo = multifocal CRUSTS  |  Herpes = grouped VESICLES — both on erythematous base
  • Topical (limited dz): Mupirocin 2% TID × 5 days (exfoliate first w/ warm cloth)
  • Systemic (extensive): anti-staph/strep oral × 7 d  —  Augmentin: <40 kg = Amox 25 mg/kg/d + 125 mg clav ÷ 2 doses
  • ≥40 kg: Amox 500 mg + 125 mg clav  |  Cutoff = WEIGHT, not age

Syphilis 1° (Primary)

  • Pathogen: T. pallidum spirochete ("Cursed corkscrews"). DPH REPORTABLE
  • Chancre onset: 3–90 days post-exposure. Sites: 85% genital / 10% anal / 4% oral
  • Oral chancre: painless, indurated, clean base, regional LAD → "THE GREAT IMITATOR"
  • DDx: SCC, aphthous, TUG, Mpox (Sully: induced border=SCC; clean+indurated=chancre)
  • Biopsy: IHC sens 64–94% vs silver stain 0–41%
  • Serology NEG up to 6 weeks → biopsy trumps serology early
  • Tx: IM Pen G × 1 dose (refer to PCP). Heals spontaneously 3–8 wks

Syphilis 2° (Secondary / Disseminated)

  • 4–10 wks post-infection (after chancre resolves). Systemic: painless LAD + rash + MSK pain + sore throat
  • Oral triad: Split papules (commissure ulcerated mass)  |  Mucous patches (white elevated, converge serpentine)  |  Condyloma lata (look like papillomas)
  • ⚠ Oral lesions HIGHLY INFECTIOUS — serpentine patches on buccal/tongue

Syphilis 3° (Tertiary)

  • Latency 1–30 years; 30% of untreated 2° progress
  • Oral: Gumma (destructive granulomatous mass — palate/tongue) + Interstitial glossitis
  • Systemic: Cardiac (arteritis, aneurysm, CHF) + CNS (Tabes dorsalis posterior column, paresis, dementia)

Tuberculosis (Oral / Secondary)

  • Pathogen: M. tuberculosis airborne. Oral TB = rare; Tongue >>> most common oral site; ALWAYS secondary to pulmonary
  • Lesion: chronic painful IRREGULAR ulcer + constitutional sx (wt loss, night sweats, cough)
  • ⚠ DO NOT BIOPSY FIRST — workup + airborne PPE first (refer ID). Sully: "protect yourself first"
  • Test: NAAT = Xpert MTB/RIF assay. Biopsy AFTER workup
  • DOT Regimen: Phase 1 8 wk = Pyrazinamide + Isoniazid + Rifampin + Ethambutol (PIRE)
  • Phase 2 16 wk = Isoniazid + Rifampin. Oral TB self-resolves with systemic tx
  • ⚠ DPH REPORTABLE (Sully: "Syphilis + TB = both mandatory reportable")

TypeAppearance / Key FactsSiteTrigger

Angular cheilitisRed, fissured, raw; commissures; loss of vertical dimension = unique triggerCommissuresVD loss, immunosupp

HHV4EBV (Epstein-Barr)"Barr has 4 letters"
HHV5CMV (Cytomegalovirus)"Cyto → cinco (=5)"
HHV6/7/8[slide cut off below fold — obtain full slide][unverified]

Primary Herpetic Gingivostomatitis (PrHGS)

  • Age: 6 months – 5 years (NO dedicated pediatric case; in Garden Variety table)
  • Prodrome: cervical LAD + chills + fever + nausea + anorexia + irritability
  • Lesion: Pinhead vesicles → coalesce → ulcer; location: KERATINIZED tissue
  • KEY: PrHGS = KERATINIZED  |  Aphthous = NON-keratinized
  • Duration: 5 days – 2 weeks
  • Adults: pharyngotonsillitis; rarely Bell Palsy (facial nerve) / vestibular neuritis

Recurrent Herpes Labialis (RHL)

  • Prevalence: 15–45% of US. = cold sore = fever blister
  • Prodrome: itching / tingling / warmth / erythema — 6–24 hrs
  • Vesicles rupture + crust within 2 days
  • ⚠ HIGHLY transmissible at vesicle rupture — avoid manipulation until scabbed

HSV Drugs — All Doses

DrugDose / Route / Freq / DurationNotes
Acyclovir suspensionKids 15 mg/kg; Adults 400 mg PO 5×/day × 5 daysMust start ≤5 days of onset!
Penciclovir 1% creamTopical q2h waking hours × 4 daysRHL / labialis
Docosanol 10% (Abreva)Topical 5×/day until healedOTC — RHL
Valacyclovir (Valtrex)1 g PO BID × 5d (HSV) | 1 g PO TID × 7d (HZO)HZO needs higher freq + ophtho
AVOID in kids: topical lidocaine (seizures) + benzocaine (methemoglobinemia)

Hutchinson Sign / HZO — EMERGENCY

  • Hutchinson sign = VZV lesion on nasal tip → V1/nasociliary nerve → corneal risk
  • HZO complications: Corneal ulceration → scarring → perforation → BLINDNESS
  • ⚠ Nasal tip HSV/VZV → MANDATORY ophthalmology referral. Ambulance-level urgency.

CMV (HHV5) Oral Ulcer

  • Context: HIV+ off ART. Case: 36yo female — large necrotic ulcers, failed 10-day acyclovir
  • ⚠ Acyclovir DOES NOT WORK on CMV — case clue for CMV over HSV
  • Biopsy: "Owl eye" nucleus (pathognomonic histology)
  • Tests: ELISA → IgG + IgM + viral culture + PCR + CD4/viral load
  • Tx: Valganciclovir 900 mg PO BID (duration not stated on slide). Immunocompetent: NSAIDs
  • Prevalence: 83% of population (older age, women, low SES, foreign birthplace)
  • #1 cause nonhereditary sensorineural hearing loss

Measles (Rubeola)

  • Transmission: airborne. Case: 6yo unvaccinated — fever 103°F + cough + rhinorrhea + conjunctivitis
  • Oral: Koplik spots = PATHOGNOMONIC — bluish-white pinpoint plaques on BUCCAL MUCOSA, 70% of pts
  • Appear 1–2 days BEFORE rash — key early diagnostic sign
  • 3 C's: Cough + Coryza (rhinorrhea) + Conjunctivitis
  • Rash: head → spreads down (cephalocaudal). Photophobia present
  • Infectious window: 4 days BEFORE to 4 days AFTER rash onset
  • PEP: MMR within 72 hrs OR Ig within 6 days
  • Vit A: 200,000 IU for children ≥12 months (hospitalized children)
  • Complication: encephalitis 1/1,000
  • ⚠ DO NOT TREAT IN DENTAL CLINIC — N-95 + negative pressure room mandatory. DPH REPORTABLE.

Mumps (Endemic Parotitis)

  • Case: 20yo college student — bilateral parotid swelling, fever, sour food pain, trismus, orchitis
  • 10–30% have NO parotid swelling (counterintuitive!)
  • Serious complications (orchitis, meningitis, encephalitis) can occur WITHOUT parotitis
  • CNS affected: ~50% (even without parotitis)
  • Gold standard Dx: RT-PCR from buccal/oral swab
  • IgM caveat: may FAIL in vaccinated patients despite active infection
  • Contagious: 1–2 days before sx → 14 days after clinical resolution; saliva virus: 7d before to 9d after sx onset
  • Incubation: 16–18 days (range 12–25)
  • Orchitis: 3–10% postpubertal males / Subfertility: 13%
  • Hearing loss / encephalitis: now <1% (prevaccine era = major)
  • 3rd MMR dose in outbreak: 61–88% additional protection
  • Management: avoid SOUR FOODS (sialagogue); analgesics/antipyretics; bed rest; warm/cool compresses

EBV Mucocutaneous Ulcer (MCU)

  • Case: 46yo post-renal transplant (tacrolimus + mycophenolate × 3 yrs) — rapidly enlarging ulcer, B-symptoms
  • Frame: "Localized, self-limited lymphoproliferative disorder with excellent prognosis"
  • ~45% regress spontaneously / NO disease-related deaths
  • 91% have immunosuppression: canonical 3 = methotrexate, azathioprine, cyclosporine (case used TAC + MMF)
  • Age-related type: median age ~77 years. Most common site: Oropharynx 62%
  • Appearance: sharply circumscribed, shallow ulcer. Mimics: malignancy, ANUG, traumatic ulcer
  • Dx: Biopsy + EBER in situ hybridization (EBER ISH) — required
  • Distinguish from lymphoma: Normal LDH + normal IL-2R + EBV DNA <1,000 copies/mL
  • Management: observe for regression OR reduce immunosuppression; chemo rarely needed; re-biopsy recurrences

PATHOGNOMONIC MASTER (L1)

  • Koplik spots (bluish-white buccal plaques, 1–2d BEFORE rash) → Measles
  • "Owl eye" nucleus on biopsy → CMV
  • Honey-colored crusts "cornflakes glued" → Impetigo
  • Serpentine mucous patches → 2° Syphilis
  • "Cursed corkscrews" spirochetes on biopsy → T. pallidum / 1° Syphilis
  • Split papules at commissures → 2° Syphilis
  • Gumma (palate/tongue) + Tabes dorsalis → 3° Syphilis
  • Condyloma lata (look like papillomas) → 2° Syphilis (NOT HPV)
  • Kissing lesion palate mirror → Median rhomboid glossitis (Candida)
  • Sharply circumscribed shallow ulcer in immunosuppressed → EBV MCU
  • Hutchinson sign (nasal tip) → V1/nasociliary → HZO/blindness risk

ORAL = FIRST SIGN (L1)

  • Koplik spots → appear BEFORE measles rash (early dx window)
  • Chancre on lateral tongue → 1° Syphilis (may be first presentation)
  • Mucous patches + split papules → 2° Syphilis before systemic workup
  • Oral CMV ulcer in HIV+ off ART → CMV reactivation (failed acyclovir = clue)
  • EBV MCU → marker of immunosuppression (transplant, iatrogenic, age-related)
  • Keratinized gingiva vesicle-ulcers in 6mo-5yr child → Primary herpetic gingivostomatitis
  • White plaques that WIPE OFF after inhaler use / abx course → Pseudomembranous candida
  • Large irregular painful tongue ulcer + night sweats + cough → TB (not SCC — no raised borders)

AVOID RULES / DENTAL RED-LINES (L1)

  • TB suspected: NEVER biopsy first — workup + airborne PPE + ID referral first
  • Measles: NEVER treat in dental clinic — N-95 + negative pressure room mandatory
  • HSV nasal tip / Hutchinson sign: MUST refer ophthalmology — HZO/blindness risk
  • Lidocaine (topical) in kids: avoid — seizure risk
  • Benzocaine (topical) in kids: avoid — methemoglobinemia
  • CMV in HIV+: acyclovir will FAIL — must use valganciclovir 900 mg PO BID
  • EBV MCU biopsy alone insufficient: must add EBER ISH to distinguish from lymphoma
  • Augmentin dosing: use WEIGHT not age — cutoff 40 kg, not adult/pediatric age
  • Syphilis: serology in first 6 wks: unreliable — use biopsy + IHC
  • Candida "-azoles": check LFTs for all (clotrimazole ↑LFTs 15%; fluconazole hepatotox)

DRUG CROSS-REFERENCE (L1)

  • Mupirocin 2% TID × 5d — Impetigo (topical, limited dz)
  • Augmentin × 7d — Impetigo (systemic, extensive); <40 kg: 25 mg/kg/d ÷ 2; ≥40 kg: 500 mg
  • IM Pen G × 1 — 1° Syphilis (refer PCP)
  • DOT PIRE × 8 wk → IR × 16 wk — TB (P=Pyrazin, I=Isoniazid, R=Rifampin, E=Ethambutol)
  • Clotrimazole 10 mg 5×/d × 10d — Candida (HIGH SUGAR; LFTs ↑15%)
  • Nystatin 5 mL QID × 10d — Candida (swish-swallow; Candida-specific; low sugar)
  • Fluconazole 100–200 mg PO QD × 7–14d — refractory Candida (check LFTs)
  • Mycolog II BID — Angular cheilitis (nystatin + triamcinolone)
  • Miconazole 2% gel QID × 10d — Denture stomatitis (to intaglio)
  • Acyclovir 400 mg 5×/d × 5d (kids 15 mg/kg) — HSV; start ≤5d onset
  • Penciclovir 1% q2h waking × 4d — RHL topical
  • Valacyclovir 1 g BID×5d (HSV) | 1 g TID×7d (HZO, ophtho)
  • Valganciclovir 900 mg PO BID — CMV oral ulcer (HIV+ immunosuppressed)
  • MMR ≤72h OR Ig ≤6d — Measles PEP
  • Vit A 200,000 IU — Measles, children ≥12 mo

Section 05

Paraneoplastic Pemphigus / PAMS

Tier 3 Awareness — BUT has Board Favorite malignancy list and 3 red-box callouts

☠

PAMS — Paraneoplastic Autoimmune Multiorgan Syndrome Tier 3 Board Favorite

Rare, highly fatal autoimmune blistering disease — ALWAYS associated with cancer
60–90% Mortality — most deaths within first year
~45% Oral lesions are the FIRST sign
~100% Have oral involvement
41–70% Ocular involvement

PAMS is what happens when a cancer (most often lymphoma or leukemia) tricks the immune system into attacking the body's own epithelial proteins. The immune system makes antibodies called anti-plakin antibodies that attack desmoplakin, envoplakin, and other cytoskeletal proteins everywhere — mouth, eyes, skin, lungs. The cancer is either already known or hidden (occult). Finding PAMS in the mouth may literally be the first sign that cancer exists in the body at all.

"Always associated with an underlying neoplasm (known or occult)"

"Oral lesions are the FIRST sign in ~45% of patients – often precedes cancer diagnosis"

"Mortality: 60–90% – most deaths within first year"

Associated Malignancies Board Favorite

MalignancyFrequencyNotes
Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma~40%Most common association
CLL (Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia)~20%
Castleman Disease~18%Most common in Asia; benign but still triggers PAMS; surgically removable → better prognosis
ThymomaLess common
Solid tumors (carcinomas, sarcomas)~16%

The 5 Mucocutaneous Phenotypes

PAMS is a shape-shifter. Its skin and mucosa lesions can look like five different diseases simultaneously — making diagnosis a nightmare if you don't already know to look for it. The oral lesions are the most consistent feature (nearly 100%) and also the most treatment-resistant.

  • Pemphigus-like: flaccid blisters, acantholysis (= classic PNP)
  • Pemphigoid-like: tense blisters, subepithelial clefting
  • Erythema multiforme-like: targetoid lesions, lip crusting
  • Graft-versus-host disease-like: interface dermatitis
  • Lichen planus-like: lichenoid papules

Characteristics by Organ System

Oral (Nearly 100%)

  • Painful tongue ulcers — more common than in PV
  • Lip erosions extending to vermilion border with hemorrhagic crusting (SJS-like)
  • Pharyngeal, laryngeal, esophageal involvement common

"Severe, painful stomatitis – intractable and treatment-resistant"

Respiratory — Major Cause of Death

  • Frequency: 6% European, 20% Japanese, 70% pediatric
  • More common with Castleman disease
  • Often irreversible; presents as progressive dyspnea, cough

"Bronchiolitis obliterans – autoantibodies attack respiratory epithelium" — major cause of death in PAMS.

Skin (Polymorphous)

  • Can mimic PV, BP, EM, lichen planus, or GVHD — often simultaneously
  • Palmar/plantar bullae — uncommon in PV, helpful distinguishing feature
  • Lichenoid papules and plaques

Ocular (41–70%)

  • Cicatrizing (scarring) conjunctivitis — similar to MMP
  • Symblepharon formation, fornix shortening
  • Can lead to vision impairment

Other Mucosal Sites

  • Genital (erosions, ulcers)
  • Nasal (crusting, erosions)
  • GI tract involvement possible

2021 Revised Diagnostic Criteria

Criteria TypeRequirementsFindings
Major Need all 3, OR 2 major + both minor
  • Mucous membrane lesions ± cutaneous involvement
  • Concomitant internal neoplasm
  • Serologic evidence of anti-plakin antibodies
Minor Both needed if only 2 major met
  • Acantholysis and/or lichenoid interface dermatitis on histopathology
  • DIF showing intercellular and/or basement membrane staining

Prognosis Notes

  • Causes of death: underlying malignancy, bronchiolitis obliterans, sepsis from immunosuppression
  • Rituximab does NOT work as well for PAMS as it does for PV
  • Better prognosis: Castleman disease (surgically removable), lichenoid pattern, oral-only initial presentation

Treatment — Supportive

  • Dexamethasone 0.5 mg/5 mL solution
  • Clobetasol 0.05% gel; Fluocinonide 0.05% gel
  • Magic mouthwash; Lidocaine 2% gel before meals
  • Avoid crunchy, spicy, acidic, hot foods
  • Chlorhexidine 0.12% soak to prevent secondary infection
  • Lanolin or petroleum jelly for hemorrhagic crusting
  • Refer for nutritional evaluation if unable to eat
DENTIST'S ROLE

Recognize: severe intractable stomatitis + vermilion involvement + polymorphous skin ± known malignancy. Refer urgently to oral medicine / dermatology / oncology. Trigger cancer workup — PNP/PAMS may be the first sign of occult malignancy. Oral lesions are first to show and last to go.

📷
PAMS Clinical Photos — Slide 56
Four images: (1) severe oral stomatitis with tongue/labial involvement — raw, hemorrhagic, diffuse; (2) facial involvement with massive crusting and erosions perioral and periocular; (3) scattered blistering lesions on the back (polymorphous skin phenotype); (4) lichenoid/targetoid skin lesions. Source: Manupati, S., & Paymaneh, F. (2022, August). Paraneoplastic pemphigus. DermNet.

Master Comparison: Vesiculobullous Diseases

PV vs MMP vs PAMS — side-by-side
Feature Pemphigus Vulgaris (PV) Mucous Membrane Pemphigoid (MMP) PAMS
Antibody Target Desmoglein 3 (mucosal) ± Desmoglein 1 (skin) Basement membrane zone proteins (BP180, BP230, laminin 332, type VII collagen) Anti-plakin antibodies (desmoplakin, envoplakin, periplakin)
DIF Pattern "Chicken wire" intercellular IgG — intraepithelial Linear IgG, IgA, and/or C3 at BMZ — subepithelial Intercellular AND/OR BMZ staining — mixed pattern
Split Level Intraepithelial (desmosomes destroyed) Subepithelial (BMZ attacked) Mixed / polymorphous
Nikolsky Sign POSITIVE NEGATIVE Variable (often positive)
Blister Type Flaccid bullae → rupture quickly → large erosions Tense bullae; desquamative gingivitis Polymorphous — can mimic PV, BP, EM, LP, GVHD
Oral Presentation >50% present with oral lesions FIRST Oral 85%; desquamative gingivitis most common Nearly 100%; severe intractable stomatitis; first to show, last to go
Ocular Risk Less common than MMP Conjunctivae 61–70%; symblepharon; BLINDNESS risk 41–70%; cicatrizing conjunctivitis; symblepharon
Associated Neoplasm No direct cancer association Anti-laminin 332 positive → 25% have adenocarcinoma ALWAYS cancer-associated (NHL 40%, CLL 20%, Castleman 18%)
Treatment First-Line Rituximab + prednisone (dose by PDAI score) Topical steroids; dapsone; refer ophthalmology for ALL patients Supportive only; treat underlying malignancy; rituximab less effective than in PV
Prognosis Significant morbidity; manageable with immunosuppression Chronic; conjunctival scarring irreversible Mortality 60–90%; most deaths within first year
⚗

Master Comparison: OLP vs Lichenoid Reaction vs Dysplasia with Lichenoid Features

The prof called this a "Diagnostic Pitfall" — exam bait
Feature True OLP Lichenoid Contact Reaction Dysplasia w/ Lichenoid Features
Distribution Bilateral symmetric Often unilateral, adjacent to restoration Unilateral or asymmetric
Location Posterior buccal mucosa, ventral tongue Buccal mucosa adjacent to amalgam/composite High-risk sites: tongue, floor of mouth, soft palate
Epithelial Architecture No atypia No atypia Bulbous rete pegs, loss of stratification
Clinical Behavior Waxes and wanes Resolves if allergen removed Progressive — does NOT spontaneously improve
Pathognomonic Finding Wickham striae (bilateral, posterior buccal) White striae adjacent to restoration None — requires biopsy
Malignant Potential Low (erosive OLP: monitor annually) Very low if allergen removed HIGH — biopsy urgently

Diagnostic Pitfall (Prof's words): Dysplasia with lichenoid features can look identical to OLP clinically. Red flags that push toward dysplasia: unilateral, high-risk site (floor of mouth, ventral tongue, soft palate), progressive behavior. BIOPSY is the only way to tell them apart.

🔥

Master Comparison: EM → RIME → SJS → TEN (Severity Spectrum)

Board Favorite territory — know the BSA cutoffs and triggers cold
Feature EM Minor EM Major / RIME SJS TEN
BSA Involvement <10% (minimal) Minimal/absent <10% BSA >30% BSA
SJS/TEN Overlap N/A 10–30% BSA = overlap zone
Primary Trigger HSV (~70%) Mycoplasma / other respiratory pathogens Drugs (~85%) Drugs (~85%)
Skin Lesions Raised 3-ring target lesions; acral distribution Minimal/absent skin lesions Flat macules → blisters → sloughing; trunk distribution Flat macules → blisters → diffuse sloughing; trunk
Mucosal Involvement None or 1 site Always prominent ≥2 sites (oral/ocular/genital) Nearly 100% Nearly 100%
Typical Patient Young adults Children/adolescents Any age; drug exposure 1–3 weeks prior Any age; drug exposure 1–3 weeks prior
Mortality Self-limiting Self-limiting 1–5% 25–50%
Recurrence Yes — HSV-driven; suppress with valacyclovir Can recur with re-exposure Less recurrent (drug-triggered) Less recurrent (drug-triggered)
SJS <10% BSA · Mortality 1–5%
SJS/TEN OVERLAP 10–30% BSA
TEN >30% BSA · Mortality 25–50%
⚠

Easy-to-Confuse Pairs

The four most commonly mixed-up entities — one key differentiator each

RAS vs Herpes (HSV): RAS = nonkeratinized mucosa, no fever, no vesicle stage, no HSV on serology, no systemic prodrome. HSV = keratinized mucosa (hard palate, attached gingiva), fever + lymphadenopathy, vesicle cluster THEN ulcer, responds to acyclovir. The slide literally uses this as the differential diagnosis comparison table.

OLP vs Lichenoid Contact Reaction: OLP = bilateral, posterior buccal, not adjacent to restorations, waxes/wanes. Lichenoid = often unilateral, directly adjacent to metal/composite restoration, resolves when restoration replaced. In exam vignettes: if it's adjacent to an amalgam, it's lichenoid.

PV vs MMP: PV = intraepithelial, Nikolsky POSITIVE, chicken-wire IgG on DIF, desmoglein target, flaccid bullae. MMP = subepithelial, Nikolsky NEGATIVE, linear IgG/IgA/C3 at BMZ on DIF, basement membrane target, tense bullae. Mnemonic: "PemphigoiD = DEEP (Hades), Nikolsky negative."

SJS vs TEN: Same disease, different severity. SJS = <10% BSA. TEN = >30% BSA. The trigger (drug) and mechanism are identical. The BSA cutoff is the ONLY difference on an exam question. Both: flat macules → blisters → sloughing on trunk. Stop the culprit drug immediately — that is the dentist's most important intervention.

💊

Master Drug List — Prof-Highlighted Across the Deck

Cyan-highlighted drugs from reference tables + Board Favorite lists

SJS/TEN High-Risk Drugs Board Favorite

Onset typically 1–3 weeks after starting new medication. Memorize this list cold.

Sulfonamides (TMP-SMX) β-lactams Fluoroquinolones Carbamazepine Phenytoin Lamotrigine Phenobarbital Allopurinol NSAIDs / COX-2 inhibitors

HIGHEST RISK

Allopurinol = highest absolute risk for SJS/TEN. Carbamazepine, lamotrigine, and phenobarbital are the AEDs to memorize.

Drug-Induced Gingival Hyperplasia

Phenytoin (anticonvulsants ~50%) Cyclosporine (immunosuppressants 25–70%) Nifedipine (CCBs 6–25%) Amlodipine (CCBs 6–25%)

Cyclosporine + nifedipine (common in transplant patients) = synergistic effect → more severe hyperplasia than either drug alone.

Corticosteroids — Prof-Highlighted (Cyan)

Clobetasol propionate 0.05% (Group 1 — super-high) Fluocinonide 0.05% (Group 2 — high) Dexamethasone elixir 0.5 mg/5 mL (not classified — soak-and-spit delivery)

RIME Treatment

Valacyclovir 500 mg BID ≥6 months — for recurrent EM (HSV-driven)

PAMS Supportive Care

Dexamethasone 0.5 mg/5 mL solution Clobetasol 0.05% gel Fluocinonide 0.05% gel Lidocaine 2% gel before meals Chlorhexidine 0.12% soak

📌

Pathognomonic Anchors

One finding = one diagnosis. If you see it, you know it.
Wickham Striae Bilateral posterior buccal white lacy lines → OLP
Migratory Lesion Depapillated patch that moves over days → Geographic Tongue (Erythema Migrans). "The migratory nature is pathognomonic and distinguishes it from malignancy."
Linear IgG/IgA/C3 at BMZ DIF showing linear band at basement membrane zone → MMP (subepithelial)
"Chicken Wire" IgG DIF showing intercellular IgG throughout epithelium → Pemphigus Vulgaris (intraepithelial)
3-Ring Target Lesion Raised concentric rings (dark center + pale ring + red outer ring) → Erythema Multiforme
Sloughing >30% BSA Full-thickness epithelial detachment >30% body surface area → TEN
Nikolsky POSITIVE + Cancer Hx Intraepithelial split + known/occult malignancy → PAMS (not PV alone)
Purple Papules on Wrists "Purple polygonal pruritic papules on wrists or ankles" → OLP (cutaneous manifestation)
Michel Solution Transport medium for DIF biopsy specimens → If the biopsy goes in formalin, the DIF is ruined. This is a classic exam trap.

Both antibodies = mucocutaneous PV

Foundational Concept Anchors
Hypersensitivity I = IgE-mediated (minutes) · Type IV = T-cell (24–72h) · Pseudoallergic = non-immune mast cell (no sensitization)
Subepithelial blister = Nikolsky NEGATIVE · Intraepithelial = Nikolsky POSITIVE
Patch testing = gold standard for Type IV contact allergy
Michel solution = DIF transport · 10% formalin = H&E
Refer ALL MMP patients to ophthalmology – even if asymptomatic
PV >50% present with ORAL lesions FIRST – you may be the first to see it
PAMS oral lesions are the FIRST sign in ~45% – may precede cancer diagnosis
Drug gingival hyperplasia: meticulous plaque control is the most important intervention
Stop the suspected drug immediately – most important intervention in SJS/TEN

Crammable Recap

Every prof-highlighted item + notecard candidates — QA-verified facts

This is the notecard. Color legend: RED = rules / red flags · BLUE = diseases / entities · GREEN = treatments / drugs · PURPLE = numbers / stats. Use this to write your double-sided 5″×8″ handwritten card.

SIDE A · OPMDs & benign HPV lesions
OD 530 · Epithelial Pathoses & SCC · Side A
HPV BENIGN
Papilloma: HPV 6/11, soft palate, solitary (98%), pedunculated, vaccine-covered
Condyloma: HPV 6/11 ~75%, multiple, sessile, STI, vaccine-covered
Verruca vulgaris: HPV 2/4, lip/perioral, auto-inoculation, NOT vaccine-covered
Heck disease: HPV 13/32, multifocal, flat, NOT vaccine-covered
RULES (red flags)
39.6% of progressors had NO dysplasia on first biopsy
Erythroplakia ~90% already severe dysplasia/CIS/SCC at biopsy
Erythroplakia median time to cancer = 3.7 mo (leukoplakia = 25 mo)
PVL/PL 48–50% MT (annual rate ~9.3%, 8× conventional)
Tobacco + alcohol = ~30× multiplicative
UV NOT cause of intraoral SCC or oral melanoma
Biopsy RED over white · deep for verrucous
Tumor seeding from biopsy = MYTH (~0.00012% FNA)
HPV+ benefit = oropharynx ONLY, NOT oral cavity
NUMBERS
US 2024: 36,600 new / 12,230 deaths
>50% present with mets; rising 1.8%/yr
Oral tongue 41.7% #1 site
Stage 5-yr OS: localized 85% → reg 70% → dist 40%
Fanconi OR ~40.6
Clinical sensitivity 60%, specificity 62% for cancer
OSF MT 5.1%, ~36 mo to cancer
OLP / OLL ~1.4% MT each
SCC: >90% oral malignancy, 7th worldwide, age ~66, M:F 2–4:1
OPMDs
Leukoplakia = white plaque, dx of EXCLUSION (rule out: frictional keratosis, candida, lichen planus, WSN)
5-yr MT by grade: No dysp 2.2% → mild 2.6–11.9% → mod 4.1–8.7% → severe 29.2–32.2%
Mild dysp: 18–39% spontaneous resolution; annual MT ~2%/yr
Erythroplakia = red velvety; dx of EXCLUSION (rule out: atrophic glossitis, MRG, plasma cell gingivitis, erosive OLP)
TWO STATS: ~50% MT rate; ~75–90% already severe dysp/CIS/SCC at 1st biopsy
PVL: F:M 2:1, gingiva, “ring around collar”, non-smoker, 48–50% MT
PVL major criteria: ≥2 sites, enlargement, recurrence after tx
OSF: areca nut → arecoline → fibroblast EMT → HIF-1α → SNAIL → cancer; trismus, 5.1% MT, 36 mo
OLP: bilateral, Wickham striae, sawtooth rete + band lymph + Civatte bodies
OLL 3 types: drug reactions + contact reactions + oral lichenoid dysplasia (higher MT)
CHEMOPREVENTION
Imiquimod 5% = TLR-7 agonist → innate immunity; Phase I/II USC
ALA-PDT = ALA → protoporphyrin IX → light → ROS; 66–100% CR
13-cis-RA 67% CR · Vit A 300k IU 52% CR · Fenretinide 87% CR
Bleomycin 0.5–1%: 25–40% CR
Curcumin 3.6g/d: 67.5% vs 55.3% placebo RCT (p=0.03)
Beta-carotene MAY INCREASE risk in smokers (CARET)
RP2 (modified HSV-1): Phase II BWH/DFCI (intralesional)
ONYX-015: 37% histologic resolution · rAd-p53: 72% clinical response
Calcipotriene ± 5-FU: NCCN preferred cutaneous field
DYSPLASIA TX
Scalpel = gold standard (margins; OR 3.6 if positive)
CO2 laser: HR 0.14 MT; 52.6% recurrence; ≥3mm margins
Diode laser: 93.5% CR; 6.5% recurrence at 39 mo
Cryo: 100% regression; mean 3.1 tx; <2cm²
ALA-PDT for multifocal/large field; 8.3% MT at 3yr
Margins: 5 mm peripheral, ≥2 mm deep
Severe dysplasia/CIS → excise + Q3-mo lifelong
SIDE B · SCC, AJCC, HPV-SCC, verrucous, melanoma
OD 530 · Epithelial Pathoses & SCC · Side B
SCC OVERVIEW
>90% oral malig, 7th worldwide, age ~66, M:F 2–4:1
Sites: lateral/ventral tongue 42% > FOM 16%
Rising 1.8%/yr; younger pts often no trad risk factors
~50% non-ulcerated — induration is the key sign
Field cancerization: ~3–5%/yr 2nd primary risk
WARNING SIGNS
Non-healing ulcer >2–3 wk
Exophytic mass or indurated area
Red/white patch that persists
Bleeding / numbness / pain
Fixation to structures
Firm fixed non-tender cervical LAD
REFERRAL WORKUP
Urgent referral → OMFS / H&N onc
Incisional biopsy (histo confirmation)
CT contrast and/or MRI (staging)
PET-CT (distant mets)
Multidisciplinary tumor board
AJCC 8 T-stage
T1: ≤2 cm AND DOI ≤5 mm
T2: up to 4 cm AND DOI ≤10 mm
T3: >4 cm OR DOI >10 mm
T4a: cortical bone, IAN, FOM, skin
T4b: skull base, ICA encasement, pterygoid plates
AJCC 8 N-stage
N1: 1 ipsi ≤3 cm, ENE−
N2a: 1 ipsi >3–6 cm OR any ENE+ ≤3 cm
N2b: multi ipsi ≤6 cm
N2c: bilat/contralat ≤6 cm
N3a: any >6 cm
N3b: any other ENE+ — worst
ENE+ → ~2× recurrence; gets adjuvant CRT
HPV+ OPSCC vs HPV− vs OCSCC
HPV+ OPSCC: tonsil/BOT, p16+, 5-yr ~80–85%, younger
HPV− OPSCC: smoker, ~40% OS (NOT 50–55%)
HPV+ OCSCC: NO survival benefit
E6 → degrades p53; E7 → inactivates Rb; p16 piles up
Gardasil 9: 6, 11, 16, 18, 31, 33, 45, 52, 58
VERRUCOUS CA
Low-grade SCC, slow, exophytic warty
Virtually NEVER metastasizes (pure form)
Cause: chronic smokeless tobacco
Dx: DEEP biopsy — need to see pushing border
Tx: wide excision; no node dissection in pure form
Mixed verrucous + conventional → treat as conventional
ORAL MELANOMA
~50% hard palate — #1 site
UV is NOT the cause (skin = UV; mucosa = unknown)
c-KIT 20–30%, NRAS; BRAF rare
5-yr OS 15–25% — worst oral malig
~15% amelanotic (pink/red, no pigment) worse prognosis
c-KIT+ → imatinib; PD-1 inhibitors for advanced
RISK MULTIPLIERS
Tobacco: 5–9× · Alcohol: 3–9×
Tobacco + Alcohol: ~30× (multiplicative)
Smokeless / betel: ~5–8×
Fanconi: OR ~40.6
Dyskeratosis cong: ~1000× baseline
HPV-16: ~15× for OPSCC (NOT oral cavity)
EXAM TRAPS
Heck NOT in Gardasil 9 (HPV 13/32)
UV NOT cause of intraoral SCC or oral melanoma
Erythroplakia > leukoplakia — biopsy red part
Tumor seeding myth — biopsy first (~0.00012% FNA)
~50% SCC non-ulcerated
p16 IHC ONLY reliable in oropharynx
Verrucous needs DEEP biopsy
Bilateral symmetric Wickham → topical steroid first
Amelanotic melanoma ~15% (NOT 25%)
HPV− OPSCC OS ~40% (NOT 50–55%)
TRANSFORMATION TRIAGE
Non-homogeneous: 4.2–6.5× higher risk
Lateral tongue: 12.7% vs 6.6% overall
FOM: 2.1× higher
Size >200mm²: 2–4× higher
Female sex paradox: higher MT despite lower incidence

Last-pass cram trio: (1) every “white patch + red component” → biopsy the red. (2) Tobacco + alcohol is multiplicative ~30×. (3) HPV+ OPSCC is a different disease from HPV+ OCSCC — survival benefit is oropharynx-only.

QA-verified critical facts — verify in master: Amelanotic melanoma rate = ~15% (not 25%). Erythroplakia has TWO metrics: ~50% MT rate AND ~75–90% already severe dysplasia/CIS/SCC at first biopsy. HPV− OPSCC 5-yr OS = ~40% (not 50–55%).

Practice Question from Lecture

This case was presented in the slides — review it for exam prep

Patient: 77 y.o. male with history of HLD, CAD (s/p CABG x4), CVA (2012), osteomalacia, T2DM without insulin (on metformin), chronic inflammatory demyelinating polyneuritis (with R side weakness, on IVIG weekly, temazepam), OSA (uses CPAP), gout, psoriatic arthritis, BL reduced hearing, glaucoma, BPH, anemia (iron deficiency and other).

Chief Complaint: Tongue pain

Histopathology report (prior biopsy): RIGHT TONGUE LESION BIOPSY — Squamous mucosa with ulceration and granulation tissue. Negative for dysplasia.

Image: TBD — Clinical photo: right lateral tongue whitish/erythematous ulcerated lesion with irregular borders, central ulceration covered with fibrin, surrounding erythema.

Exam Tasks (from the slide):

  • Describe the lesion — location, size, color, shape, border, surface texture
  • DDx — differential diagnosis
  • Tests — what workup would you order?
  • Plan — management approach

Key considerations for this patient: (1) He has glaucoma — narrow-angle glaucoma is a contraindication to sialagogues. If his dry mouth contributes to tongue ulceration, you cannot simply prescribe pilocarpine. (2) He has extreme polypharmacy (>10 medications) — his dry mouth is almost certainly medication-induced. (3) The histopath shows ulceration + granulation tissue, negative for dysplasia — this is reassuring but doesn't eliminate the need for follow-up. (4) Clotrimazole (Mycelex) has sugar — not appropriate for diabetic patients (from quiz review slide).

Quick Reference

Exam Day Cheat Sheet

The highest-yield facts from this lecture, condensed for last-minute review.

Topic Key Fact
Hyposalivation vs XerostomiaHyposalivation = objective (measurable). Xerostomia = subjective (sensation). Only hyposalivation causes dental damage.
Flow rate thresholdsUnstimulated: ≤0.1 mL/min. Stimulated: ≤0.5–0.7 mL/min.
Polypharmacy>3 meds/day = OR 2.9 for dry mouth. Oxybutynin has highest risk (OR 18.9).
Radiation caries patternIpsilateral, cervical, smooth surfaces of mandibular anteriors, maxillary molars.
Radiation dose thresholdsParotid: >25 Gy irreversible. Submandibular: >39 Gy irreversible.
Challacombe Scale1–3 mild, 4–6 moderate, 7–10 severe.
Pilocarpine5 mg TID. Non-selective, highest M3 affinity. Contraindicated in asthma, glaucoma, cardiac disease.
Cevimeline30 mg TID. Selective M1+M3. Fewer side effects than pilocarpine.
M3 receptorSalivary/lacrimal/sweat glands + smooth muscle. The target of sialagogues.
Sjögren demographicsMiddle-aged female, 9:1 F:M, 0.5–1% prevalence.
ACR-EULAR scoring≥4 points. Biopsy = 3, Anti-SSA = 3, Schirmer/ocular = 1, flow = 1, Anti-SSB = 1.
Seronegative SjögrenUp to 30% can be antibody-negative. Don't rule it out.
Lymphoma risk5–10% lifetime NHL risk (MALT). 7–19× general population. Red flag: unilateral enlargement.
Sialolithiasis80–90% submandibular. Pathognomonic: meal-time pain + swelling. Not all stones are radiopaque.
Acute sialadenitisS. aureus #1. Tx: Augmentin or clindamycin. Refer if airway/sepsis/immunocompromised.
MucoceleLower lip 60–70%. Bluish, fluctuant, painless. Extravasation type (no epithelial lining). Excise with feeding gland.
Avoid in dry mouthCaffeine, alcohol (beverages + mouthwash), Vaseline/Aquaphor.
Clotrimazole warningMycelex has sugar — not appropriate for diabetic patients.

Dr. Henderson's Quiz Review Reminders

From the opening slides — exam technique tips
  1. Always ask about medical history and medications
    • Open-ended questions first
    • Specific questions second
    • Don't bias yourself!
  2. Read questions carefully — sometimes she asks for several things
  3. Serpiginous = white snakelike borders (for geographic tongue)
  4. Provide measurements for EVERY lesion
  5. Describe each lesion independently
  6. Clotrimazole (Mycelex) has sugar and is not appropriate for diabetic patients
